# Release readiness: burndown

Goal, invariants, owner decisions and resume protocol: [README.md](README.md).
Predecessor: [font fidelity everywhere burndown](../font-fidelity-everywhere/burndown.md).
Counts, open items and the work queue: `pnpm report:release` (`-- --now` for the queue only, `-- --live` with
the live state of the linked pull requests).

Status values: `todo`, `in-progress`, `review` (PR open), `done` (merged and
evidence linked), `descoped` (owner decision, issue linked). Dates are UTC. The
table is parsed by [report.mjs](report.mjs); keep one row per item, the six
columns below, and a status from the list.

## Now

**RR-68 closure — 2026-10-15 12:14 UTC:** Root accepts actual Core #681 `c7eef3e7` (exact reviewed tree `e79142b1`) and the complete **12:12:25 UTC** metadata census: **zero open PRs, zero drafts and 37 issues** across eight repositories. The original Dependabot sweep is satisfied under the retained 2026-10-03 exact-advisory exception; Dev #85 remains a conditional future trigger. This follow-up records the scoped closure and does not claim its own future merge. [Accepted basis and current evidence](../../evidence/rr-68-sweep-closure-2026-10-15/README.md).

The work queue: one row per open item, saying who works on it, what blocks it and the next action. Update the
row whenever an item changes hands or state, and delete it when the item closes (the report flags closed items left
here). Parsed by [report.mjs](report.mjs): `pnpm report:release -- --now`, or `--live` for the live state of every
linked pull request. RR-68 and owner-boundary observations updated 2026-10-15 UTC; other rows retain their dated evidence.

[pptx-dev#83](https://github.com/Data-Advantage/pptx-dev/pull/83) merged 2026-10-02 (`98743c7`): an allowlist beta (`UNDERSTAND_DECK_ALLOWLIST`, Clerk user ids, fails closed, 403 `understand_not_enabled_for_user`), a capability endpoint (404 while the feature is off), a gateway-refusal fallback (one refusal skips the model for the rest of that request), ZDR dropped from the launch requirements and the `.env.example` key value blanked. Owner decisions of 2026-10-02: allowlist beta first, ZDR not required, the current per-user caps kept, a $50 per month hard spend limit on the Vercel AI Gateway. Updated 2026-10-05: pptx.dev now runs on a Cloudflare Worker, so the three flags (`UNDERSTAND_DECK_ENABLED`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_UNDERSTAND_DECK`, `UNDERSTAND_DECK_ALLOWLIST`) are Worker variables / build variables, not Vercel settings, and `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` is set as a Worker secret; the site runs on the Clerk development instance and a dev Convex deployment for now (owner decision 2026-10-05, no production users yet). What is left is owner-only: set the three flags as Worker / build variables (redeploy for the `NEXT_PUBLIC_` one), confirm the AI Gateway limit, rotate the old key if the removed value was real; then the supervisor runs the live eval once and the live fixtures are recorded (the merged recordings are hand-authored stand-ins); the privacy copy (D5) only before widening |

The Vercel bypass secret issue [opf#299](https://github.com/OpenPresentation/opf/issues/299) is no longer needed and can be closed; no Vercel setting, secret or `PREVIEW_SMOKE_ENABLED` variable is required. Previews per branch on Cloudflare are CF-17 in the [Cloudflare hosting program](../cloudflare-hosting/README.md) |

Release-note behaviour changes (each reaches users who upgrade to 0.13): (1) strict chart numbers: in non-strict documents a chart text cell such as `"12%"`, `"$5"`, `"(5)"`, `"1,234"` or `"Q1"` becomes a gap with a `chart-value-not-numeric` warning, where the exporter used to strip the text and write 12 or 5 (`"1e6"` is now 1000000, not 16); (2) a two-column scatter plots column 2 against the row numbers; (3) an unknown dataset or an unknown mapping column fails validation (`dataset-unknown`, `dataset-field-unknown`, `chart-mapping-unknown-column`). The schema change is additive (`check:breaking` reports 0); documents without the new fields export byte-identically (29 renderer digests, 21 PPTX golden digests, 805 slides of core examples unchanged) |

([opf-pptx#127](https://github.com/OpenPresentation/opf-pptx/issues/127));

`dataResolver` hook; [opf#240](https://github.com/OpenPresentation/opf/issues/240));

## Acceptance criteria

**RR-01 Release.** opf-pptx 0.11.8 (carrying #126) and the CLI 0.9.2 are
published from merged `main` through the trusted-publishing workflows on core
0.11.4, with registry artifact and provenance verified. The CLI keeps
`engines.node` `24.x` (owner Node 24 decision; pptx-dev#26 is closed as
superseded by pptx-dev#32, and it concerned pptx-dev's own `@pptx/cli`). The
sites adopt the versions.

**RR-02 pptx.dev CI.** `master` is green on ubuntu and windows over repeated
runs, with no assertion relaxed and the original failures preserved. The open
checkboxes of opf#88 are each resolved or recorded as a separate issue.

**RR-03 FF review items.** Each of the ten items is `done` with dated evidence,
or stays `review` with exactly what is missing and the RR item that covers it.

**RR-04 Cross-platform.** The pairwise matrix (FF-09) runs in CI on ubuntu,
windows and macos through a packed TypeScript consumer (FF-10), including runs
with no system fonts, and export bytes do not depend on host fonts, OS, locale
or timezone (FF-11).

**RR-05 Native sample.** The FF-12 criteria hold: at most 12 decks opened
read-only, including CJK and RTL, `Presentation.Fonts` naming only the chosen
families. Supervisor-run; evidence merged under `docs/evidence/`.

**RR-06 Editor.** For each gallery dimension the editor switches the value,
applies it as a patch, undoes it exactly and refreshes the preview (FF-16), and
design-level options are editable.

**RR-07 Preview polish.** Code blocks highlight by language, the metric trend
renders, and all 54 background pattern presets draw in the preview and export
as the same native pattern fill (parity audit agrees).

**RR-08 Import details.** `fromPptx` restores author arrays, run `ColorRef`
colours (`var:` and scheme references), item descriptions and the bullets form,
or emits a specific diagnostic; export-then-import tests cover each.

**RR-09 Hard line breaks.** Rich text with hard line breaks (`a:br`) re-imports
as the one authored payload, not several paragraphs.

**RR-10 SVG images.** SVG pictures export as native PowerPoint SVG pictures
(`asvg:svgBlip`) with a raster fallback `a:blip`, import back to the SVG source,
and draw in the preview. A native open confirms the picture (supervisor).

**RR-11 Native header/footer.** Slide-number, date and footer content export as
native PowerPoint header/footer: slide master and layout placeholders of type
`sldNum`, `dt` and `ftr` on each slide, `p:hf` flags that match, so Insert >
Header & Footer owns them, and re-import keeps the OPF furniture. Preview,
export and re-import agree; native confirmation is supervisor-run.

**RR-12 Vector PDF.** The renderer writes a PDF whose text is vector and
selectable, using the bundled fonts, deterministically.

**RR-13 Raw signals.** `fromPptx` returns the layout and style signals of a
third-party deck (placeholder roles and geometry, theme and master styles,
shape order and kinds) as plain data with no network or model calls.

**RR-14 Understand this deck.** pptx.dev reconstructs a third-party PPTX into
structured OPF from the RR-13 signals with AI assistance and a review step the
user controls; nothing runs in the libraries.

**RR-15 Gate.** The browser-versus-Fontkit variable-font width gate is 0.15 px
(from 0.1 px) in opf-render, with the decision recorded in the test and the
docs; no other tolerance changes.

**RR-16 0.01 pt snap.** Either composed font sizes are quantized to 0.01 pt (rounding down) in
core with the golden hashes regenerated and the lockstep floor raised, or the
parity harness treats a difference below PowerPoint's own 0.01 pt resolution as
equal; the decision is recorded and the two `list-6x-heading-title-center`
layouts become `perfect`.

**RR-17 Font long tail.** FF-41 to FF-46, FF-05 and FF-13 are `done` or
explicitly descoped by the owner, per their font burndown criteria.

**RR-18 Hygiene.** One issue per descoped item (video, `ChartDataSource`) with
current behaviour, what full support needs and evidence; roadmap issues #87 and
#88 carry a current status comment; opf-render#24 and opf#213 point at their RR
items.

**RR-19 Housekeeping.** No stale worktrees, merged branches or scratch folders
remain from the program; user checkouts are never switched or cleaned by agents.

**RR-21 Slide management.** The editor deletes, duplicates, reorders (drag and
keyboard), hides and sections slides, and offers a slide sorter and an outline
view; each action is one patch with exact undo and the preview refreshes.

**RR-22 Autosave and restore.** The editor autosaves to browser storage,
restores the last session and warns before unsaved changes are lost; the three
sites adopt it without changing their look and without sending content anywhere.

**RR-23 Downloads.** The editor and the sites download the deck as vector PDF,
PNG and SVG next to PPTX, from the same rendering the preview uses.

**RR-24 Data grid.** A grid edits chart data and tables; table rows and columns
insert, delete and move as single undoable patches.

**RR-25 Editor tools.** Deck-wide find and replace (with undo as one patch),
image crop and focal point, and a usable mobile layout.

**RR-26 Conversions.** `@openpresentation/opf/convert` converts list and table,
timeline and table, metric set and table, promotes and demotes list levels,
parses quote attribution, turns an image into a slide image or background,
wraps content in a group or region and splits or merges slides. The functions
are pure and deterministic, and the editor applies each as one transaction.

**RR-27 CLI.** `opf render`, `opf export pdf|png|svg|pptx` and `opf import
deck.pptx` run offline on the supported Node versions, with deterministic
output and tests on ubuntu, windows and macos.

**RR-28 Player.** An embeddable `<opf-deck>` web component plays a deck
(slideshow, keyboard navigation, speaker view with plain-text notes) and the
openpresentation.org example pages use it. No transitions or builds (deferred).

**RR-29 Audit.** `opf audit` reports contrast, overflow, missing alt text,
reading order and font usage with stable rule ids and exit codes, and the editor
shows the same findings in a Review panel.

**RR-30 Markdown.** A core subpath and CLI convert Markdown and outlines to and
from OPF with a documented mapping and a round-trip test.

**RR-31 Diff, format and patch.** `opf diff` and merge report and combine
changes between two OPF files, `opf format` normalises a file, and one core
JSON-patch module is used by both the CLI and the editor.

**RR-32 Templates and variables.** A template is an incomplete OPF file.
Variables cover text, number, date and image or asset values (not only
colours) inside text and image fields. `opf fill` and decks from data fill a
template, the editor offers a fill UI, and validation reports unfilled
variables. The schema change is additive.

**RR-33 Numbered lists.** The spec has numbered lists (style, start value,
nesting) and preview, PPTX export (`a:buAutoNum`) and import agree.

**RR-34 Footnotes, citations and captions.** The spec has footnotes, citations
and captions; preview, export and import agree or report a specific diagnostic.

**RR-35 Chart options.** Charts take axis titles, legend position and data
labels; the preview and the native PPTX chart agree and re-import keeps them.

**RR-36 Single-series chart colour.** A single-series bar or column chart exports
with one series colour (the data points do not each get their own), the parity
harness counts `c:dPt` fills of classic chart series, and a native open confirms
the colour (supervisor).

**RR-37 Packed install on Windows.** The CLI's bin script installed from the
packed tarball runs on Windows: the staged bytes are the bytes npm installs, the
file is named when the installed runtime differs, and a Windows checkout packs
the published bytes (LF pinned for dist, src and scripts in opf-render).

**RR-38 Arabic Typesetting scale.** The preview draws Arabic Typesetting at
PowerPoint's size through a font policy `sizeAdjust` multiplier (0.64), with the
decision and measurement recorded; export is unchanged.

**RR-39 CJK and Thai line breaking.** Core line breaking uses dictionary word
breaking for Thai, Lao, Khmer and Myanmar and East Asian kinsoku (no closing
punctuation at the start of a line, no opening bracket at the end), and the
preview and export agree; a probe deck confirms PowerPoint's own breaks
natively (supervisor). Descoped from the first release on 2026-10-02 with
[opf#278](https://github.com/OpenPresentation/opf/issues/278), which states the
current behaviour, what full support needs and the evidence (README Definition
of done 2). It moves geometry, so it ships with a core release and
raised lockstep floors.

**RR-40 CI/CD.** A measured study of the seven repositories (durations, queue, failure
and flake rates, minutes per OS, redundancy, critical paths of a change and of the
release train) and a target pipeline with a prioritised roadmap, each item with its
expected saving, risk and migration ([ci-cd.md](ci-cd.md)); up to three safe quick
wins merged without changing rulesets, gates or publish trust settings.

**RR-45 to RR-53 CI/CD roadmap.** Each item is done when it is merged with the
before-and-after measurement its study section names ([ci-cd.md](ci-cd.md),
roadmap): RR-45 `packages` wall clock about 12 min p50 with identical commands;
RR-46 no changelog or script-list conflicts in a week of PRs; RR-47 a measured
pass rate per browser suite and a quarantine file honoured by CI; RR-48 every
`main` commit verified through the queue; RR-49 site builds verified on their
Vercel preview before merge; RR-50 no hand-edited pins and no pin off `main`;
RR-51 a lockstep train run by the workflow with artefacts and provenance
verified; RR-52 two golden-moving renderer PRs merge without a textual
conflict; RR-53 the core PR tier runs contract suites and the full suites run
in the queue and on schedule. No gate or tolerance is relaxed by any of them.

**RR-41 Gallery tracker.** `pnpm build:gallery-tracker` writes `gallery-tracker.json` and `gallery-tracker.md` from the committed data only (catalogs, schema, audits A and B, the latest parity run, the font tracker, native evidence, the burndown and two pinned snapshots of pptx.gallery and opf-editor); every catalog record and every gallery item is one record with the columns spec, compose, preview, export, round trip, parity, editor, gallery, native and fonts (`unknown` where no data source exists); each non-done record names its gaps, next action and link; `pnpm check:gallery-tracker` (in `pnpm test`, so in CI) fails when the committed tracker is stale; `pnpm report:release` prints the one-line summary (records, addressed, unaddressed by type). Internal only.

**RR-42 Native gallery sample.** Owner decision 2026-10-02: native evidence for every value, not a sample. Every value the gallery tracker marks `works-unverified` is named by a committed native PowerPoint evidence run (deck sources, the per-value comparison and the PowerPoint reports under `docs/evidence/rr-42-native-<date>/`), and the tracker's native column reads that run. Each failing value is listed with its failing check. Supervisor-run, read-only.

**RR-43 Unmeasured catalog records.** The 38 narratives, 8 audiences and 9 purposes without a gallery config and the 6 non-widescreen slide-size presets are measured like audit B (validate, compose, render, export, re-import, parity; `p:sldSz` for the presets) on the published packages, the result is committed and the tracker reads it, so none of them stays `unknown`.

**RR-44 Chart tick labels in parity.** The parity text check no longer reports a computed value-axis tick label as a wrapped chart label; the classifier change is recorded in the gallery-support README with before/after counts on the 26 charts, no tolerance changes, and the re-run is part of the RR-20 re-audit.

**RR-54 Chart and table data.** The contract in `docs/chart-table-data.md` is implemented in core, the preview, the PPTX export and import, and the editor: one strict chart-number rule (`chartNumber`), column number formats that reach data labels, axes and table cells, shared datasets with `fields` and `chart.mapping`, and `data.source` references; the schema change is additive, documents that use none of the new fields produce unchanged output (digests recorded in each PR), no gate or tolerance changes, and the three behaviour changes (non-strict chart text cells become gaps with a warning, a two-column scatter plots column 2 against the row numbers, an unknown dataset or mapping column fails validation) are named in the release note. A bounded native PowerPoint check (supervisor-run, read-only plus one `SaveCopyAs` of a copy per deck) shows the formats on labels and axes, Edit Data opens the embedded workbook with the column formats and no missing values, the decks open without a repair prompt, and a plain save then re-import restores the datasets, mapping and formats; failed observations stay as evidence. Done when the four pull requests (opf#376, opf-render#127, opf-pptx#171, opf-editor#92) are merged on green CI with the native evidence linked; RR-20 publishes them in the 0.13 train.

**RR-57 Agent and CI throughput.** An agent's edit is checked by Biome at the edit (`.claude/settings.json` hook), at most `OPF_AGENT_SLOTS` (default 3) heavy commands run at once on a machine whatever the worktree, `pnpm check:changed` checks a branch's changes in a fraction of `pnpm test` and names what it skipped, none of it changes what CI runs, and CI pins the pnpm version and uses TypeScript 7 everywhere the repository type checks.

**RR-58 Layout ownership.** Every layout record has one owner for its defaults (the layout, not the gallery), and image-bleed is full-bleed; the gallery follows.

**RR-59 Developer contracts.** The Codex review findings are fixed in each repository: core and CLI contracts usable as documented, bounded web font delivery and restored presentation focus in the renderer, PPTX export integrity and authored semantics, optional design in the editor's catalog controls, and executable developer starters with current guidance on openpresentation.org.

**RR-60 Markdown decks.** A `*.opf.md` deck opens in every CLI command that reads or writes a deck, and core `readDeck`/`writeDeck` read and write it, released in the lockstep 0.16.0 (core and CLI 0.16.0).

**RR-61 SVG font embedding.** Passing the fonts handle to `renderSvg` is safe everywhere: each SVG embeds only the faces its text draws (family, weight and style), `embed: "always"` keeps a face in every SVG, and `embedFonts: false` embeds none while the handle still measures. Raster goldens stay byte-identical, the vector PDF still embeds every drawn face, SVG sizes are measured before and after on the core example decks, a test shows that a slide using one family embeds only that family, and the editor drops its `previewFonts` workaround once the renderer is released.

**RR-62 CLI API.** `@openpresentation/cli/api` exports `readDeck`, `writeDeck` and `validate` (re-exported from core) and
`exportDeck(deck, { format: "pdf" | "png" | "svg" | "pptx", fonts?, catalogs? })`, which returns `{ files, findings }`, and
`importDeck(pptxBytes)`. opf-render and opf-pptx stay optional peers, loaded lazily, and a missing one is a typed
`peer-missing` error. Core becomes a real dependency of the CLI package, so an app that uses the API has one core. Every
CLI command that renders, exports or imports is a thin wrapper over the API, with unchanged output. The change is
additive and ships as CLI 0.16.0 in the lockstep 0.16.0. The package count stays at five.

RR-62 continues in the 0.17.0 lockstep (owner decisions 2026-10-09; one breaking 0.x minor, no 0.16.x patch): the file API
moves into core as the Node-only `@openpresentation/opf/node` (`convert(input, output?, options?)`, `open(pathOrBytes)` returning
the presentation, `save(deck, path, { validate })`), with the export and import engine, the lazy peer loading and a fonts handle
prepared once per process behind it; the root and every other core subpath stay browser-safe (`check:browser-safe`). Core's
`readDeck`/`writeDeck` become `parse` (returns the presentation, throws `OPFValidationError` with located findings) and
`stringify`. `@openpresentation/cli/api` is removed; the CLI is the command, with `opf convert <input> <output>` and `render`,
`export` and `import` running core's engine with unchanged flags, reports and exit codes (`render`/`export` take the format
from `--out`'s extension). render and pptx are optional peers of core and of the CLI.

**RR-63 Lighter renderer install.** pdf-lib, resvg and sharp are optional dependencies of opf-render, loaded only by the
converters that need them, and a missing one is a typed `converter-missing` error. The `@expo-google-fonts/*` packages
are optional, so a browser host installs none of them. New `@openpresentation/opf-render/png`, `/pdf` and `/html`
subpaths produce byte-identical output to today's converters (the raster and PDF goldens are unchanged). The renderer's
install contract changes; it ships as render 0.16.0 in the lockstep 0.16.0. The package count stays at five.

**RR-64 Text as outlines.** `textAsPaths: true` draws every `<text>` of a slide as glyph outlines with the handle's outline engine, so the SVG needs no font: positions, widths and shaping are the vector PDF's, outlined elements keep their attributes, `data-opf-*` trace and links and carry their text as an accessible label, and text in a colour font stays text with a diagnostic. With the option off the output is byte-identical; on the core example decks resvg draws an outlined slide like its text, and the trade-offs (no selectable text, no editing surface, no real text in a PDF made from it, lighter small text in browsers) are documented. Phase 1 of opf-render#164; the later phases are tracked there.

**RR-67 pr-gate.** `node scripts/pr-gate.mjs --merge` reports a PR that the merge queue merged as `MERGED` (exit 0), because the queue records a removal event with the reason `merged` for it and `failed_checks` or `manual` removals stay EJECTED (exit 5); only the newest report of each check counts per workflow and job name (workflow run id, then check run id, then start time), so a re-run or a newer run that went green replaces the failed one and a newer failure replaces an older success; with `--expect-head` a previous head served right after a push is re-read up to 3 times, 15 s apart, before exit 4.

**RR-20 Readiness note.** A release-readiness note for the owner (FF-15) lists
what ships, what is descoped and the evidence; the final lockstep train is
published and verified; the audits are re-run on the published packages and the
re-audit result is linked.


Owner subsequently confirmed Actions budget increased; canonical reruns on exact deployed site heads are authorized/pending. Preserve original no-code billing outcomes and wait actual required checks/default merge proof; no forced merge or extra manual deployment is implied.
**RR-85 Metric centering.** With no explicit alignment, shorthand metrics, metric blocks, nested groups and layout-template regions center horizontally within their allocated block, including the inline value/unit pair and label, description, delta and trend text. Explicit slide, deck, host, theme, layout and the direct `layoutMetric` API's `align` option retain their existing precedence and logical direction semantics. Ordinary prose, lists and table alignment is unchanged. Meaningful regression checks pass, renderer and PPTX consume the accepted alignment/positions, and the change merges through normal gates before publication with coordinated dependent core floors.

**RR-86 Keynote qualification.** Maintain reproducible current-content Keynote lifecycle, editing and structure controls for the approved compatibility fixes and opt-in whole native Body paragraphs/flat bullets, with measured defaults preserved. Bound unsupported nested/description/multicolumn composition, literal footer visibility, unsupported chart paint, native fonts/wrapping and notes-field adaptations with diagnostics/evidence or explicit scoped disposition. Preserve current edits/deletions across provenance modes, meaningful negatives, default-byte controls and immutable historical fixtures. Source/package/preview success does not establish native acceptance. A real registry-native17 claim requires genuinely verified publisher/archive/runtime identity and actual Body/fallback checks; fresh Mac/native criteria remain pending and Windows stays on explicit human HOLD.

Historical evidence: the [2026-10-15 Keynote integration plan](keynote-integration-plan.md) and its observation JSON predate the monorepo and name the former separate repositories; its status text is not current.
